Robert Livingston fflstory OF DEXTER BAPTIST CHURCH Dexter Baptist Church was organized April 29, 1906, under the leadership of Elder R.G. Marsh. There were twelve charter members at Dexter when it was admitted into the Flat River Association at Hester Baptist Church between June 17, 1906, and July 14, 1906.

The first Revival was held the third Sunday in October 1906. There were five members added by baptisms and one by letter added at this time.

The first communion was held on the third Sunday in December 1906, and Dr.
Marsh was called as the first pastor for the year 1907 at $125.00.

The first pulpit chairs were donated by A. Barnes, of Henderson, and the table given by J.Robert Wood, of Oxford.

The first policy of insurance on the Church building was $800.00 in March 1909.

The first Foreign and Home Missions Committees were appointed in 1910 with the first collection of $20.00 donated to Foreign Missions in 1911. The first song books were also bought in 1910.

The pulpit stand was bought by the ladies in January 1911.

Reverend E.G. Usry became our second Pastor in 1913 and served for 24 years with the membership of 75 when he was called.

The first W.M.S. was organized in 1913 with the help of Miss Sue Kelly from Henderson. The first W.M.S. Collection for Foreign Missions was taken March 1914.
amounting to $21.80.

The first recorded orphanage offering was taken November 1919, and the first Biblical Recorder subscriptions were made October 1923.

Brother Jack Usry, son of Reverend E.G. Usry, was the first preacher to be licensed in October 1923, from Dexter. Then in September 1926 he was ordained along with a school friend, James Davis, from Bullock.

In November 1927, Dexter made her first recorded contribution of $26.00 per year to the Flat River Association for the support of an Associational Sunday School worker.

In March 1937, Dexter decided to have four preaching services per month with Reverend Lowell Sodoman as pastor at his suggestion.

The first nursery organized in April 1960.

The Vestibule and two Sunday School rooms were added in 1960-1961. The Cradle Roll and the Extension Departments were organized on November 9, 1962.

Rotation of the ordained deacons began and the first organ was bought in February 1962.

Six classrooms, the baptistry, the bathrooms and the yard light were added in 1965.

The Sanctuary was carpeted in 1966, and a discussion was held on an addition to the back of the building. In June 1967 the Church voted for the addition on the back of the building, 20 feet by 60 feet.

In 1967 the central heating system was installed. Also the first Youth Revival was held in August 1967.

On October 27-29, 1972, one of our former pastors who is a Foreign Missionary to Israel, Reverend Jimmy Burnham, led our Missionary Conference at Dexter.

PASTORS OF DEXTER BAPTIST CHURCH Rev. R. During the pastorate of Rev. J.B. Davis, a movement was started to build the present church on a site which was a gift of J.H.
Perry. Cooperative labor and contributions of the entire community, not just church members, went into the construction of the church.

Succeeding Rev. Zachariah Allen, who served as first pastor through 1843, was Rev. Elizah Hester. He served until the end of 1847. Rev. W.A. Atkinson ministered unto the people until 1873, a period of twenty-five years, this being the longest term of any pastor during the history of the church. The following have served as pastors since that time: Rev. E.F. Beacham 1873 Rev. J.A. White "" 1874 Rev. W.A. Barrett 1875-1880 Rfev. C.W. Scarboro 1881 Rev. John C. Hocutt 1882-1885 Rev. J.W. Atkinson 1886-1899 Rev. M.A. Adams , 1898-1897 Rev. J.A. Brindell ^ , 1900-1902 Rev. J.A. Stradley 1903-1906 Rev. L.L. Hudson , 1907-1910 Rev. E.M. Lassiter 1911-1912 Rev. O.W. Yates 1913-1914 Rev. H.G. Bryant 1915-1918 Rev. J.B. Davis ^ - 1919-1921 Rev. J.A. Brindell 1922-1923 Rev. E.R. Harris 1924 Rev. W.D. Dorsett 1925 Rev. A. Lamonds 1926-1927 Rev. L.J. Rainey 1928-1933 Rev. J.U. Teague , 1934-1936 Rev. W.C. Francis 1937-1941 The first Bible Society or Bible School was organized in 1847 as an auxiliary to the Flat River Bible Society. The first recorded revival meeting was held in 1849, the first Missionary Society was organized in 1879, and the first musical instrument-a church organ-was used in 1894.

Fellowship Church has always been known by its present name and has been a member of none other than the Flat River Baptist Association. In 1877, R.O.
Weathers was granted a license to preach and in 1892 David F. Lawrence was licensed and ordained into the ministry.

On April 28, 1940, the church had a centennial celebration; the above information was presented at that time as church history to that date. At that time, the membership numbered 175, the church was free of debt and was supporting as native Manchurian missionary who devoted his full time service to preaching in his own country.

Rev. L.R. Jordan began his pastorate in October 1941 and served through 1946. He was succeeded by Rev. Aaron Phipps who served until August 1948, after which Rev.
C.H. Norris was called and preached through September 1952.

Rev. R.R. Pulley was called and began serving on February 15, 1953. In 1955, four new Sunday School rooms and a study were added on the south side of the church.
The church voted to have morning and evening services every Sunday beginning in October 1956. The church continued to grow materially, as well as spiritually, and the present parsonage was bought in January 1958. Extra cemetery land was purchased from Bro. J.H. Perry and a central heating system was installed in the parsonage in 1960. A church library was started in 1961 and restrooms and three new Sunday School rooms were added. In 1962, the church voted to license John Parrott to preach. The latest addition to the building--a basement and eleven classrooms-were completed, dedicated and used for the first time on October 16, 1966. Rev. Pulley resigned effective July 1, 1966. He was away for a little over three months. The dedication services for the new educational unit was his first service after being recalled. He remained as pastor until February 1, 1971 and was followed by Rev.
R.R. Moran whose first service was on August 1, 1971. During 1971, the church sanctuary was air conditioned. In 1972, the long awaited dream of a baptistry was realized. This facility was dedicated and used for the first time on June 25, 1972.
Mrs. Ola Horton, seventy-five years of age, was the first one baptized. A new organ was bought in 1974 to enhance our music program. During the same year, two of our members, Wilson Green and Tommy Richardson, dedicated their lives to full time Christian service. Wilson has since completed his first year at Lynchburg Baptist College and recently began his second year in preparation for the ministry. Bro.
Richardson has gone back to his hpme community near Burlington, North Carolina where at last report he is still proclaiming the gospel through music.

On November 3, 1974, heirs of J.H. Perry presented the church the deed for land for additional parking space and a tract to even the boundary line at the parsonage.

Rev. Moran resigned his pastorate here on February 23, 1975. Dr. Richard Spencer, professor of New Testament at Southeastern Seminary, served as interim pastor until the calling of Rev. Tony Wilson. His first service was on July 16, 1975.
Rev. Wilson is a second year student at Southeastern. The two months that he has been with us have given us cause to look forward to the years to come, knowing that with God as our head great things are in store for us.

Compiled by: Mrs. Marie Temple The Reverend J.U. Teagae 1882-1975 .i^>^ BIOGRAPHICAL SKETCH OF DECEASED FORMER PASTOR IN FLAT RIVER The Reverend J.U. Teague Jackson Uriah Teague was born on October 8, 1882 near Taylorsville in Alexander County, North Carolina. Beloved as preacher, teacher, farmer, he was the third son in a family of ten children of Marcus M. and Katherine Munday Teague.

He was educated in the public schools of Alexander County and in Yadkin Valley Institute, and upon his graduation from Wake Forest College he became the first of the "four preacher brothers," and served in tne Baptist Ministry for approximately forty years.

In 1910 J.U. league was ordained into the ministry by Antioch Baptist Church, the offspring of "The New Meeting House", which was the first Baptist church in that entire area and was organized in 1797 by Pioneer, Rev. Edward league, who served as its pastor until he died in 1810.

During his study for the ministry Mr. Teague supplied in several rural churches which helped to finance his education and to give him pulpit experience. His first pastorate was at Thanksgiving Baptist Church in Johnston County. In his senior year he was married to Burla Elizabeth Shearin of Franklin County, North Carolina and graduated from Wake Forest College in 1915, "Cum Lauda." While pastoring at Thanksgiving, he accepted the position of Principal at Princeton High School where he taught several years before entering the Baptist Seminary at Louisville, Kentucky. Due to a tlue epedemic while there he returned to Franklin County with his wife and two sons, Jasper and Rudolph. Farming with his father-in-law, he accepted a position at Epsom High School as teacher-principal until he was called to a new field of churches in the Flat River Association where he served his longest pastorate. For nineteen years he was the pastor of Mountain Creek, Grassy Creek, Amis Chapel and Averettes. During this time there was a third addition to his family, a daughter, Mary Katherine. He served several other churches in Flat River Association for shorter periods of time.

Mr. Teague spent most of his life serving country churches in North Carolina and Virginia, conducting religious meetings and revivals throughout the country. His greatest life-purpose was to "win souls to accept Christ as their personal Saviour and to help back-sliding Christians to walk closer with Jesus Christ." Retiring in 1948 from the Powellsville, North Carolina field of churches, he moved back to his home in Henderson and continued serving the Lord as Interim Pastor at Mountain Creek Baptist Church.

Home Missions, as well as Foreign, played an important role in the ministry of Mr. Teague during his active years as pastor and throughout his years of retirement. He and Mrs. Teague continued to serve the Lord in this capacity by their help given to a pioneer Baptist mission work in the Morman City of Twin Falls, Idaho.

Dr. Charles A. Leonard, a former missionary to China and Friend of The Teagues, introduced them through correspondence to the Reverend Mr. and Mrs.
Schreckenberg, formerly of North Carolina, who revealed to them his plans to establish a Baptist Church there. Through the help of the Teagues, many friends, and former churches-and several years of struggle-Eastside Baptist Church was, built in a much needed location in Twin Falls. Also, it has sponsored another mission j church in Jackpot, Nevada. Both of these churches were built through the aid and services of North Carolina people who rallied to the call of the Reverend E.J. Jenkins, Superintendent of Missions, Box 356, Mars Hill, North Carolina. Reverend Mr. andji Mrs. Jenkins has also served as pioneer missionaries and knew of the work being j done by The Schreckenbergs.

Mountain Creek Baptist Church has contributed to Eastside Church for several years as part of their Home Mission work, and all who give consider it a God-given privilege.

The Reverend Teague was a Past Master Mason, and he was given a life-time membership in the Henderson Masonic Lodge 229. Until his death on June 19, 1975, at the age 92, he was a member of the First Baptist Church of Henderson, North Carolina.

The "living memorial fund" given to Eastside Baptist Church by many of his former churches and friends is deeply appreciated by his family and the Church.
Through these gifts in commemoration, two chairs for The Lord's Supper Table were purchased with the remainder going to the church building fund.


Mrs. Brenda Ellis of the Butner First Baptist Church gave the afternoon devotion on "What Love Is. ..and Isn't." Mrs. Carl Blake, WMU Director of the host church, extended the welcome to the 143 persons present.

The business session began with the secretary's report being given by Brenda Ellis. The minutes were accepted and approved as read. Mrs. Morgan Daniel, Treasurer, gave the financial report. Mrs. James R. Frazier, Chairman of the Budget Committee, presented the proposed budget for the coming year. Time and Place Committee Chairman, Mrs. R.T. Critcher, informed the group that the 1975 Leadership Conference would be held at the Butner First Baptist Church on September 22. She also announced that the 1976 Spring Meeting would be held at Fellowship and the 1976 Leadership Conference would be held at Tabbs Creek. The Nominating Committee's report was submitted by Mrs. Billy Tilley of the Bullock Church.

The Acteens of the Amis Chapel Church presented a puppet show, "Not All Alone," which centered its them on the Cooperative Program. It was a delightfully executed interlude, and quite entertaining!

Mrs. David Peach of the Butner First Baptist Church rendered the afternoon music.

Mrs. Sanford Daniel introduced the afternoon speaker. Miss Pat Ritchie, who is the North Carolina Director of Girls in Action and Missions Friends.

The evening devotional period which made reference to the upcoming Easter season was led by Mrs. Jerry Jarrell of the Grassy Creek Church, after which a special offering was received for the Cooperative Program. This offering amount was There were 168 persons in the evening session.

Mrs. Davis announced that the Flat River WMU had once again received recognition as a Distinguished Associational WMU for the year 1973-74. At the time of her report she also appointed a new Committee to study the By-Laws. Those persons named were Mrs. Betty Cobb, Tabbs Creek; Miss Lila Piper, Providence; and Mrs. Lillian Adcock, Mount Zion. Mrs. Davis capped upon each associational director of the various age level groups to give a brief talk about their activities for the year.

An installation service was led by Mrs. Jean C. Tilley of Bullock who likened each officer to a flower in a garden. (These officers are listed in Associational Directory.) The Enon Crusaders, a group of high school male singers from Enon Baptist, delivered the evening session's special music.

Rev. Earl Howard of Mount Zion introduced the evening speaker. Rev. Harry Byrd, a missionary to Guatemala who is at the present time on furlough. His sermon was inspiring, challenging, and quite interesting.

The meeting adjourned at 8:45 P.M. by the Rev. Bob Wainwright, Superintendent of Missions offering the benediction.
